Three separate attempts this session ran into that wall, which is why it is now stated
here rather than rediscovered a fourth time.

- [x] **[low]** engine `opt/rewrite_fuzz.rs` — a 9,000-node fixture added
      (`optimizing_preserves_rows_across_block_boundaries`, `fixture_sized`), kept as
      DIVERSITY coverage and labelled as such. Above the floor the gate changes which plans
      seed — 318 plans per 2,000 contain a seek at 24 nodes against 158 at 5,000 — so the
      plan pairs differ from the small fixtures', and a rewrite wrong only when a seed is
      DECLINED can only be caught here. Same justification `across_fixtures` already runs
      on. 2.09s at the default budget.

  WHAT IT DOES NOT COVER, each established by injecting a panic rather than assumed, and
  each recorded at the test:

  - the gate itself — perf-only, so oracle-blind;
  - the selectivity probe — `seed_fraction` is already reached by every other test, because
    `orient_is_worth_it` consults it with NO floor;
  - `exec/order.rs`'s 2,048-row block-streamed dedup and `exec.rs`'s 8,192-row streamed
    projection. Neither is reachable from generated plans at ANY fixture size. The first
    needs `streaming_chain` to accept the chain and it has no arms for the `ShortestPath` /
    `RepeatGroup` / `OptionalExpand` hops the generator mixes in; the second is cost-gated
    and never entered. These are named holes with a reason, not something size fixes.

- [~] **[low]** generating `Plan::DistinctBy` — TRIED AND REVERTED, for two measured
  reasons. It is the one IR node the generator has never emitted, so it looked like an
  obvious gap.

  1. With a PARTIAL key it is ORDER-SENSITIVE: it keeps the first row per key, and the
     optimizer may legitimately change arrival order, so the result is genuinely
     unspecified. The fuzzer reported a false failure within seconds (seed 7220: group `n4`
     raw against `n3` optimized). Confirmed as order-sensitivity and NOT a pushdown bug by
     the discriminating test — with the key covering every slot, which makes it
     content-stable, all seeds pass. This is consistent with the repo's order-is-unspecified
     policy; `dedup('a')` over an unordered stream is `SELECT DISTINCT ON (a)` with no
     ORDER BY.
  2. With a FULL key it is oracle-invisible, per the limit above: poisoning
     `distinct_by_keep` to dedup nothing leaves every seed passing.

  So the shape needs a differential (TS-vs-native) oracle. Recorded at the site so the next
  reader does not repeat the attempt.

## 28. Fuzzer: the fixture reached ONE of four adjacency dispatch paths (2026-09-27)

`for_each_nbr` picks how to serve a single-type hop from STORE CONFIGURATION, not from the
plan — so a fixture that is always in one configuration tests one branch and silently skips
the rest. A scratch probe on `fixture(1)` found it pinned in the least interesting one:

```text
has_multi_label_edges() = false
has_edge_type_index()   = false
out_typed_csr(0, T1)    = None     <- the overlay is STALE
```

The overlay is dropped by `delete_node`, which the fixture does (`i % 11 == 7`), and nothing
rebuilt it. So every fuzz run took the flat scan, and the per-type CSR path — which
item 26 had just made the PRIMARY one — had no fuzz coverage whatsoever.

- [x] **[med]** engine `opt/rewrite_fuzz.rs` — FIXED 2026-09-27. `configure_adjacency` cycles
      the four states across the sixteen fixtures of
      `optimizing_preserves_rows_across_fixtures`: fresh CSR, stale CSR + edge-type index,
      stale CSR with no index (what it always was), and multi-label edges with a fresh CSR.
      State 3 draws its secondary labels from `ETYPES`, so a generated `-[:T2]->` really can
      match an edge whose primary type is `T1` — tagging them with a label no predicate asks
      for would run the branch without testing what it decides.

  TWO OF THE THREE NEW STATES ARE GUARDED, verified by poisoning: dropping an edge from
  every per-type CSR span, and dropping one from every index bucket, each turn
  `optimizing_preserves_rows_across_fixtures` red. Both were completely silent before.

  THE THIRD IS NOT, and that is a finding about the ORACLE rather than the fixture. Removing
  the secondary-label probe from `for_each_nbr`, and removing `slice_degree`'s multi-label
  bail, BOTH leave every seed passing. The reason is structural: raw-vs-optimized cannot see
  a bug in a primitive both plans share, nor one in a fast path both plans qualify for. It
  sees a fast path disagreeing with ENUMERATION, which needs the optimizer to change which
  of the two applies — which is exactly why the CSR and index poisons are caught and the
  multi-label ones are not.

  So the multi-label class belongs to the TS-vs-native byte-identity fuzzers, which is where
  the `algo.rs` primary-label bug was in fact found. State 3 is kept because running the
  branch costs nothing and a future fast path could create the asymmetry this oracle can
  see, but the comment at the site says plainly that it is not the oracle for that class.

## 27. Filter(Scan) materialization: scoped, one part taken, the rest declined (2026-09-27)

Item 18's last open note, and the last lead of this sweep. Scoped rather than started, which
is the result.

- [x] **[low]** engine `exec.rs` — TAKEN. Every seed site (`Scan`, `NodeSeed`, both seeks, and
      their capped twins — eight in all) built its `Col` from a CLONE of the id vector and
      then read the original to fill lineage. On an untracked query, which is nearly all of
      them, that was a whole extra id vector copied for nothing — 800KB on a 200k scan. The
      lineage is now taken off the slice first and the ids are MOVED in.

  Worth **1-5% and no more**: `Filter(Scan)` on a 20% predicate 289.0us -> 275.4us, a bare
  scan projection 1044.8us -> 1029.6us, a hop off a scan flat. That is AT this repo's noise
  floor, so it is kept for being strictly less allocation and copying for identical output,
  NOT on the strength of those numbers. The comment at the site says so.

- [~] **[med]** the actual materialization gap — DECLINED, with the scope written down.

  `Filter(Scan)` is 275.4us where the scan-fallback `IndexSeek` doing the same work is
  194.6us, because the filter builds the whole 200k-row batch and then compacts a keep list
  to 40k, while the seek's fallback emits only survivors as it walks.

  GOOD NEWS FIRST: this needs NO IR change. The framing that it wants a new physical operator
  is wrong — the engine already has the operator, it IS the scan-fallback `IndexSeek`, and a
  fused scan+filter would live entirely inside `exec`'s `Filter` arm as a special case for
  `Filter { input: Scan, .. }`. No new `Plan` variant, so no codec, wasm or TS-parity
  surface.

  WHY IT IS STILL NOT SMALL: `try_filter_keep` operates on a `Batch` — it takes the batch's
  id column and indexes the storage columns by row. A fused version needs those same typed
  fast paths driven by an ID ITERATOR instead, across roughly eight arms (Str, Dict, Num,
  Bool, the edge-property path, the `Not` De Morgan path, and the two disjunction paths).
  Either they are re-expressed generically over an id source, or they are duplicated — and
  duplicated slot/predicate logic is what produced the two crossed-slot bugs and the
  `for_each_nbr`/`slice_degree` mirroring hazard already recorded here. Lineage is a second
  consumer: a tracked `Filter(Scan)` must still produce per-survivor lineage, which the
  gather currently gets for free.

  So: a ~1.4x on one shape (an unselective equality where an index exists but is declined),
  against a refactor of the vectorized filter's whole dispatch. That is the right trade to
  make deliberately at the start of a piece of work, not at the end of a sweep. Recorded here
  so the next person starts from the scope rather than from the measurement.

### Regression sweep across this sweep's twelve commits (2026-09-27)

Every measurement in this sweep was aimed at the thing being changed, so the whole bench
corpus was run at `cf090ca4` (before the first of them) and at HEAD and diffed.

NO REGRESSIONS. Three rows flagged >20% slower in the first pass were all parallel-ingest
timings, which nothing in these commits touches; re-running that group three times per side
gave baseline 477/461/460ms against head 476/465/456ms at 4 threads — the flag was one slow
sample.

TWO UNMEASURED WINS turned up, on workloads none of the commits targeted:

```text
query/gremlin  V count      5.80 ->  0.10   58x   (the O(1) live_node_count, item 20)
query/gremlin  dedup city  659.40 -> 296.60  2.2x  (the dedup filter fold, item 22)
```

`g.V().count()` answers from `live_node_count`, which used to sweep the tombstone bitmap —
so making that O(1) for the row evaluator's benefit also made a whole-graph count constant
time. Neither was predicted; both are why the corpus exists.

## 26. Exec: declaring an EDGE-TYPE index made a typed hop slower (2026-09-27)

The third index in this sweep to cost time by existing, after the range seed (item 18) and
the equality seed (item 18). Found by measuring a decline rather than by suspecting a bug.

- [x] **[high]** engine `exec.rs` `for_each_nbr` — FIXED 2026-09-27. A single-type hop can
      read its edges from either of two structures, and the order between them was wrong:

  ```text
  the per-type CSR overlay   a contiguous slice of one shared array, addressed by offsets
  the edge-type index        a per-node HashMap<etype, Vec<Adj>> — a hash lookup plus a
                             pointer chase into a small scattered allocation
  ```

  The index was tried first. E82, 50k nodes at degree 32 across 8 edge types, wanted type 1
  in 32:

  ```text
  -[:RARE]-> count       no index 151.0us   indexed 306.8us -> 147.7us
  -[:RARE]-> projected   no index 279.7us   indexed 502.0us -> 269.1us
  ```

  So declaring the index cost **2.03x** on a count and 1.79x on a projection; both are now at
  parity with not declaring it. `slice_degree` had inherited the same precedence and is fixed
  in lockstep — it must mirror `for_each_nbr` or it reports a degree for a different edge set.

  The index remains the FALLBACK, and not vestigially: the CSR overlay is dropped by any
  adjacency write and rebuilt in bulk, while the index is maintained incrementally, so on a
  write-heavy store the CSR returns `None` and the index is what keeps a typed hop off the
  flat scan.

  WHY THE SWAP IS SOUND: both are built by walking `out_adj` in order, so they yield the same
  edges in the same order and the precedence is a pure cost choice. Pinned by
  `the_type_csr_and_the_edge_type_index_agree_edge_for_edge`, which records every
  (neighbour, edge) pair for every node, direction and type with the overlay fresh, then
  invalidates it with a write and demands the index produce the identical sequence. Reversing
  the index bucket's iteration turns it red.

  TWO SURVIVING MUTANTS, and they are the point rather than a gap: removing the index
  fallback, or removing the CSR path, changes no answer — each falls through to another
  structure that yields the same edges in the same order. That all three agree is exactly
  what makes this a cost decision, so the benchmark covers it and no unit test claims to.

### The two declines, measured and LEFT ALONE (2026-09-27)

Both were deliberate and documented; neither had a number attached. Now they do.

- [~] A type DISJUNCTION (`-[:RARE|T0]->`) must not union buckets — that reorders against the
  flat stored-order scan and would break byte-identity. It costs NOTHING: 1072.3us
  unindexed against 1013.2us indexed, because both walk the adjacency anyway. No reason
  to revisit it.

- [~] A graph with ANY multi-label edge skips BOTH structures, since each keys on an edge's
  primary label and would miss a `:Y` match on an `[X, Y]` edge. This one is expensive —
  2290.7us against 151.0us, **15x** — and one secondary label anywhere disables it for the
  whole graph. NOT attempted: serving the primary matches from the CSR and then sweeping
  for secondary ones would emit them in two groups rather than in stored order, which is
  the same byte-identity objection that rules out unioning disjunction buckets. Closing it
  properly needs a per-type structure that carries secondary labels, which is a storage
  change, not a dispatch change. The index state is irrelevant here (2290.7us against
  2245.0us with it), so this is the cost of the decline itself.

## 25. Planner: a pattern with nothing above its last hop was never considered (2026-09-27)

Item 24 left this open and called adding it "not a one-liner" because of a suspected drift
hazard. The hazard turned out to be benign, and there is now a test that would catch it if
it were not.

- [x] **[high]** engine `opt.rs` — FIXED 2026-09-27. `orient_scan` reached a pattern only
      through a `Filter`, so a pattern with NOTHING above its last hop — rooted at an
      `Expand` — was never considered. That is what
      `MATCH (a:L)-[:T]->(b:M)-[:T]->(c) WHERE b.k > v` lowers to when the far node is
      written without a label, which is an entirely ordinary way to write it. E81:

  ```text
  middle-of-2, far node unlabelled    648.8us -> 152.6us    4.25x
  ```

  It ends up the FASTEST row in E81 — faster than the labelled spelling at 212.1us — because
  there is no residual label check above the hop.

  The candidate loop is now `best_pivot`, shared by the `Filter` and `Expand` arms, so there
  is one definition rather than two.

  THE FEARED HAZARD, and why it was wrong: the two functions decide the pattern/residual
  boundary in OPPOSITE orders — `orient_scan`'s `Filter` arm recurses first and treats
  itself as the pattern only if that fails, while `orient_apply`'s tries the rewrite on
  itself first. Since `split_chain` accepts a non-pivot predicate above the pattern, they
  can absorb the filter on different sides. That is harmless: whichever side takes it, the
  subtree returns rebuilt with `open = true` and the parent renames only its OWN
  expressions, so the decompositions differ in where the residual predicate sits, not in the
  rows or the rename. Verified rather than argued — the mutation that makes `orient_apply`'s
  `Expand` arm claim `open = true` WITHOUT rewriting (exactly the half-applied rename that
  was feared) turns both new tests red.

- [x] **[med]** engine `opt/rewrite_fuzz.rs` — the generator now sometimes OMITS the
      far-side predicate, and this one IS load-bearing, unlike the interior-pivot generator
      change rejected in item 24. With a far-side predicate always present every generated
      pattern is `Filter`-rooted, so the new arm had no fuzz coverage at all: poisoning its
      pivot left every seed passing before the change and fails after it. That is the whole
      argument for it.

  A coverage floor went in at `> 0` rather than a fraction, after measuring that the
  intersection which actually guards the arm — Expand-rooted AND re-seeded — is 5 of 2,000
  deterministic seeds, against 3 before. A proportional floor would have been a number
  picked to pass. Two weaker floors were tried and discarded first: one on the shape alone
  (163/2000 against 69/2000 — satisfied either way, so it guarded nothing) and one at
  `n / 50` (both sides clear it). What is worth catching is the count reaching zero.

## 24. Planner: a predicate on a pattern's MIDDLE node could not seed (2026-09-27)

Audit item 10's last open piece, and the last substantial lead in this sweep.

- [x] **[high]** engine `opt.rs` — FIXED 2026-09-27. Orientation reverses a chain so a
      FAR-side predicate seeds it, but a predicate in the MIDDLE is not helped by
      reversing — whichever end you start from it is still in the interior. It is helped by
      SPLITTING: seed the middle, walk backwards to the start, then forwards to the end.
      E81, 100k nodes x 5 edges, range index on `age`, `count(*)`:

  ```text
  query                     before     after   hand-split equivalent
  middle-of-2, forwards     678.8us   203.0us       205.0us          3.34x
  middle-of-3, forwards    1523.3us  1055.9us      1090.5us          1.44x
  far-end, forwards          n/a      141.0us       137.9us          unchanged
  ```

  Each forwards spelling now costs what the hand-written split of the same query costs.

  THE PERMUTATION IS THE ONE ALREADY THERE. New position of original slot `s` is `at - s`
  for `s <= at` and `s` above it — which is exactly `reverse_slots` with `at` as its pivot.
  So `reverse_chain` became `split_chain(plan, at)` with orientation as the `at == far`
  case, every consumer rename in `orient_scan`/`orient_apply` works unchanged, and there is
  ONE definition of the rename rather than two that can drift (they drifted once already).
  `reverse_chain` is deleted, not left as a wrapper.

  Far-end pivots are tried FIRST and still win: reversing keeps the chain LINEAR, which is
  what the count and degree fast paths recognize, while a split gives the seed two branches.
  A test pins that a far predicate still reverses rather than splitting.

  FOUR MUTATIONS, all caught: disabling interior splits (2 unit tests), taking the right
  branch's `from` raw instead of through the permutation (2 unit tests AND the fuzzer),
  not flipping the left branch's directions (6 tests, including the pre-existing orientation
  ones), and placing predicates by their original slot (the fuzzer). The unit tests project
  EVERY slot and compare against the raw plan, because this is the third slot-permutation
  rewrite here and the previous two each shipped a crossed-slot bug a `count(*)` fixture
  could not see.

  A FIXTURE LESSON, paid for again: the first version of all three tests used 4-6 node
  graphs and NONE of them re-seeded — including the plain far-end reversal. Orientation
  fires only when the pivot's share of the graph is under 15.4%, and one matching node in
  six is 16.7%. The failure of the far-end case is what identified the gate rather than the
  new code.

  THE EXPAND-ROOTED CASE IS DONE TOO (2026-09-27) — see item 25.

- [~] **[low]** engine `opt/rewrite_fuzz.rs` — a deliberate generator change to create
  interior pivots (making an intermediate predicate seekable sometimes) was tried and
  NOT KEPT: measured 58/2000 plans containing a split with it against 54/2000 without,
  and the poison that matters is caught either way. The shape already arrives because
  pushdown relocates a seekable conjunct of a mixed `And` onto a middle slot.

  A density FLOOR was added instead, because that coverage is incidental and could be
  diluted away by an unrelated generator change — which is exactly how a mutation for an
  earlier bug stopped being caught once. The floor also documents what the fuzzer cannot
  do here: disabling the split preserves rows, so a row-comparing fuzzer is blind to it.

## 23. Exec: the DISTINCT sweep, arm by arm — one win, three rejections (2026-09-27)

Item 22 gave `try_distinct_scan_prop`'s `Dict` arm a local whole-graph loop instead of
`scan_visit`'s closure, worth 1.78x. This is the obvious follow-up asked for every column
type, and the answer is NOT uniform — most of this entry is what did NOT work.

- [x] **[low]** engine `exec/distinct.rs` `Bool` arm — FIXED 2026-09-27. **2.11x**, 141.8us
      to 67.2us on a two-valued column over 100k nodes (E80). A bool column does the least
      work of any arm and was the SLOWEST of the low-cardinality ones, which is what pointed
      at the call shape rather than the work.

- [~] **[low]** the `Str` and `Num` arms — NOT TAKEN. Measured 1.05-1.07x, inside this
  repo's noise floor. A version giving all four arms the loop through a shared macro
  made the `Dict` arm **1.14x WORSE** (83.6us to 95.3us): four textual copies of the body
  inflate the function enough to cost the arm that was already fast. Only the arms that
  measured a win have it, and the comment at the `Bool` arm says why the others do not.

- [~] **[low]** `low_card_int_bitset`'s two sweeps — NOT TAKEN. Same shape of change, and it
  measured nothing: `DISTINCT n.bucket` over a 50-value column went 202.7us to 196.0us.
  The cost there is two passes over memory, not the call shape. Recorded at the function.

  The control worth keeping next to that: disabling the bitset entirely and falling back to
  the `FnvSet<u64>` path measured **575.8us** for the same query, and 2052.6us against
  879.6us for a 100k-distinct column. The bitset is emphatically right; only the idea of
  inlining its sweeps was wrong.

- [~] **[med]** engine `exec/fastpath.rs` deep-chain `count(*)` (lead: the `count_hops >= 2`
  branch walked the adjacency with `for_each_nbr` to learn the final hop's degree, where
  the shallow branch has used `matching_degree` for a while) — CHANGED, but the change
  measured **NEUTRAL** and is kept only for consistency:

  ```text
  deep 2-hop, 1% seed      44.5us -> 43.5us
  deep 3-hop, 1% seed     491.7us -> 446.5us
  deep 2-hop, unseeded   1752.4us -> 1797.9us
  ```

  All inside the noise floor. The deep-chain cost is propagating the INTERMEDIATE frontier
  (O(hops x edges)), not counting the last hop, so there was never much here to win. E80
  carries these rows so the next reader does not re-derive it; the comment at the site says
  it is a consistency change, not a win, and that a future run making it look like one
  should be treated as a fixture problem.

## 22. Exec: Gremlin `dedup()` ran a filter the dedup already performs (2026-09-27)

The last `spelling_probe` cliff. `spelling_probe` now reports NO PERF CLIFFS — down from
four at the start of this sweep.

- [x] **[med]** engine `exec/fastpath.rs` `try_distinct_dict_col` — FIXED 2026-09-27.
      `values(k)` drops an element whose property is ABSENT, while `RETURN DISTINCT n.k`
      treats the absence as a null, so the two spellings genuinely differ and Gremlin
      carries an extra filter GQL does not:

  ```text
  GQL  Distinct   { Project { Scan } }
  GRM  DistinctBy { Project { Filter(PropertyExists) { Scan } } }
  ```

  Hand-built plans showed that filter was the WHOLE gap before anything was changed —
  `DistinctBy { Project { Scan } }` produced the same answer in 86.9us against 270.1us with
  it. And it is redundant with work the dedup already does: the dict path tests `present[i]`
  per row anyway. So it is folded in, and an absent row is skipped instead of bailing off
  the fast path (which is why the `spotty` column, a third absent, gains the most).

- [x] **[low]** engine `exec/distinct.rs` `try_distinct_scan_prop` — FIXED 2026-09-27, same
      commit, to stop the fix above from simply INVERTING the cliff: with Gremlin at 80us
      and GQL's `DISTINCT` at 148us, GQL became the slow spelling. Its `Dict` arm now runs
      the whole-graph sweep as a local loop instead of through `scan_visit`'s closure.

  Two tidier fixes were tried on `scan_visit` itself and REJECTED with numbers: rewriting
  its `for_each(&mut f)` as plain loops changed nothing (164.0us), and `#[inline(always)]`
  made `count(DISTINCT n.dept)` WORSE, 80.6us to ~148us. Only the local loop moves it, so
  only the `Dict` arm (the one measured) has it.

  E79 (new), 100k nodes:

  ```text
  spelling                                  before     after
  GRM values('dept').dedup()                253.5us    80.5us   3.15x
  GRM values('dept').dedup().count()        260.7us    81.4us   3.20x
  GRM values('spotty').dedup() [1/3 absent] 176.9us    66.0us   2.68x
  GQL RETURN DISTINCT n.dept                148.1us    83.2us   1.78x
  GQL RETURN DISTINCT n.spotty              131.1us    49.1us   2.67x
  GQL count(DISTINCT n.dept)                 82.3us    81.4us   flat   <- must not move
  ```

  The two spellings land at 80.5us and 83.2us — parity, not a reversal.

  THREE SURVIVING MUTANTS, all explained. Weakening the see-through's `fslot == slot` or
  `fkey == key` guard changes no answer, because Gremlin's lowering MERGES any additional
  filter into an `And` — a bare mismatched `PropertyExists` is unreachable from the surface
  today, so both guards are defensive and no test can cover them. Removing the
  skip-an-absent-row `continue` only falls back to the general path, which is correct and
  slower. The mutations that DO change answers — treating any filter, or an `And`-shaped
  one, as the redundant one — are both caught by
  `a_dedup_does_not_swallow_a_filter_on_another_property`.

  A METHOD NOTE, because it cost two rounds: the first fixture was five rows, and
  `dict_encode` keeps a column as `Str` unless its distinct values are at most half the
  rows, so the dict path was never reached and every mutant survived. The second round's
  "path never runs" reading was my own error — `cargo test` captures `eprintln!` from
  PASSING tests, so the probe printed nothing. Poison the branch and watch which tests go
  red; do not trust a silent trace.

## 21. Exec: a `count(*)` over a typed hop walked edges to learn a degree (2026-09-27)

Item 19 folded the frontier `hasLabel`, leaving both `spelling_probe` cliffs at ~2.0x
against a "degree-sum floor". This closes them, and the floor turned out not to be one.

- [x] **[med]** engine `exec.rs` `matching_degree` — FIXED 2026-09-27. It read a raw
      adjacency length only when the hop's type set covered EVERY edge type, and walked
      the adjacency for anything narrower. But a hop is spelled `-[:KNOWS]->` far more
      often than untyped, and a single wanted type is ALSO a contiguous slice — the
      per-type CSR span, or the edge-type index bucket. Extracted as `slice_degree`, which
      mirrors `for_each_nbr`'s dispatch branch for branch.

  This is the escape the E68/E69 rejected-lever note asks for by name: _"Do not re-open
  this without a way to answer an endpoint predicate WITHOUT visiting edges."_ A universal
  label is not an endpoint predicate at all, and a single-type degree is a slice length.

- [x] **[med]** engine `exec.rs` `sparse_or_dense` — FIXED 2026-09-27, same commit, and the
      larger half. Seeding a count frontier folded the seek's ids through an `FnvMap` to
      collapse duplicates. A range seek walks a BTreeMap of distinct values and a node holds
      one value per key, so the ids arrive STRICTLY INCREASING and there is nothing to
      collapse — the hashing was half the query. One comparison an id detects that exactly;
      a bucket with duplicates still takes the map.

  The two branches yield the same pairs in a different ORDER. That is already true of
  `Sparse` against `Dense`, so every consumer of a count frontier is order-insensitive by
  construction — they sum, take a min/max under a total order, or insert into a set. All
  five consumers were checked. Nothing may emit rows in frontier order.

  E77, 100k nodes at degree 5, seeded 1% — the three stages of this and item 19:

  ```text
                                   floor   universal label   1%-sized label
  original                         16.4us       143.9us          105.0us
  after folding the last hop       16.4us        33.9us           40.0us
  after the degree read            13.3us        28.7us           33.2us
  after skipping the id fold       13.3us        15.8us           20.9us
  ```

  **9.1x** on the universal label, **5.0x** on a 1%-sized one, and 1.23x on the floor
  itself, which was never a floor. `spelling_probe` is down from 4 perf cliffs at the start
  of this sweep to ONE (`DISTINCT` vs Gremlin `dedup`, 1.60x, cross-language).

  A SURVIVING MUTANT, explained rather than papered over: deleting `slice_degree`'s
  edge-type-index branch changes no answer, because it falls through to the CSR branch and
  both describe the same edge set. It is a choice between two correct readings, so the
  benchmark covers it and no unit test claims to. The guards that DO carry answers — the
  multi-label-edge bail and the `Dir::Both` exclusion — each turn tests red, the latter
  five of them.

  `a_degree_read_agrees_with_walking_the_adjacency` is the standing guard against drift:
  `slice_degree` is only correct while it mirrors `for_each_nbr`, which has four branches
  chosen on store configuration, so the test compares the two directly across four store
  shapes, three directions, five want-set shapes and every node.

## 20. Exec: the row evaluator's `hasLabel` priced a probe as one bitset entry (2026-09-27)

This is audit item 11's `[med]` "~19ns/row label-bucket binary search", found to be a
mispriced threshold rather than an unavoidable cost. It sits in the ROW EVALUATOR, so it
applies to every `hasLabel` the engine evaluates, not only to counts.

- [x] **[med]** engine `exec/evaluator.rs` `Expr::IsLabeled` — FIXED 2026-09-27. Two
      implementations exist, a membership bitset and a per-row `binary_search` of the
      label buckets, chosen by `rows >= total_bucket`. That rule prices a probe as ONE
      bitset-fill entry. A probe is ~19ns a row; a fill entry is ~0.34ns. So the bitset
      was declined on frontiers where it wins outright. The estimate now compares three
      measured costs — probe per row, allocation per node, fill per id.

  Plus a short-circuit that needs no estimate: `IsLabeled` is ANY-of, so a label every
  LIVE node carries is a tautology. Buckets hold only live ids, so recognizing it is a
  length comparison. It skips both implementations.

  E78 (new), projecting a hop's frontier over 100k nodes at degree 5:

  ```text
  frontier label      seed     before      after
  every node (taut.)    1%     148.7us     63.9us    2.33x
  every node (taut.)   10%    1194.0us    508.1us    2.35x
  50% of nodes          1%     122.4us     61.6us    1.99x
  50% of nodes         10%    1393.7us    454.8us    3.06x
  1% of nodes           1%      28.9us     29.6us    flat   <- must not regress
  1% of nodes          10%     232.1us    225.8us    flat   <- must not regress
  ```

  The 1% rows are the guard, not a win: a small frontier against a small bucket should
  keep probing. Two earlier attempts cost them — forcing the bitset unconditionally, and
  the `live_node_count()` call below before it was made O(1).

- [x] **[low]** engine `store.rs` `live_node_count` — FIXED 2026-09-27, required by the
      above. It counted the tombstone bitmap on every call, which is fine once a query and
      not once a ROW: the tautology check asks for it per `IsLabeled` evaluation, and a
      100k-bool scan there cost 3us a call, enough to erase the win on small frontiers. Now
      an O(1) read of a counter maintained on every write to `deleted` (35 call sites
      benefit). `deleted` is written in six places; the counter is maintained at each, and
      a store test walks every reachable path comparing the cache against a fresh recount.

  Two of the six turned out to be UNREACHABLE, which mutation testing showed by leaving
  those mutants alive: `delete_node` early-returns on an already-deleted node, so its
  increment is unconditional, and undo replay clears a tombstone before the matching pop,
  so the pop never sees one. Both are noted at their sites instead of being guarded as if
  tested. The one live path — the UNSET on rollback of a delete — does turn the test red.

  A THIRD surviving mutant is also correct: inverting the bitset/probe threshold changes
  nothing, because both implementations are right. The threshold is a performance choice,
  so E78 covers it and no unit test claims to.

## 19. Exec: a frontier `hasLabel` built a path-count map it never needed (2026-09-27)

- [x] **[med]** engine `exec/fastpath.rs` `try_frontier_count` — FIXED 2026-09-27.
      `spelling_probe` had flagged two 8.4x groups since it was written, and both were
      this one thing: a frontier `hasLabel` above a hop feeding `count(*)`.

  ```text
  MATCH (a:Person)-[:KNOWS]->(b)         WHERE b.age > 98   16.4us
  MATCH (a:Person)-[:KNOWS]->(b:Person)  WHERE b.age > 98  143.9us
  ```

  The orientation work had blamed a residual label check for defeating the degree-sum
  `count(*)` shortcut. That was the wrong diagnosis. The labelled shape routes to a
  DIFFERENT fast path, `try_frontier_count`, which built a per-node path-count map for
  the LAST hop and then summed it — a ~5,000-entry `FnvMap` scatter at ~16ns an insert,
  for a total the map was never needed to produce. `count(*)` wants the sum, not the
  distribution, so the last hop is now streamed: per node still carrying paths, count
  its label-matching neighbours and accumulate `paths * matches`. Every EARLIER hop
  keeps its map, which is what stops a deep chain from enumerating an exploding path
  multiset.

  Plus an exact short-circuit: a label every LIVE node carries is a tautology
  (`IsLabeled` is ANY-of), so both the membership bitset and the per-neighbour test are
  skipped. Buckets hold only live ids, so the test is a length comparison.

  E77, 100k nodes at degree 5, seeded 1%: **16.4 / 143.9 / 105.0us** becomes
  **16.5 / 33.9 / 40.0us** — 4.2x on the tautology case, 2.6x on a 1%-sized label. Both
  `spelling_probe` cliffs fall from 8.4x to ~2.0x. The residual 2x is honest: this path
  WALKS the adjacency where the degree-sum shortcut reads a span off the CSR offsets.

  TWO WRONG TURNS WORTH RECORDING, both from measuring the wrong thing:

  1. First fix aimed at the membership bitset, swapping it for a `binary_search` per
     neighbour the way the row evaluator does for a small frontier. It made the query
     WORSE, 142.3us to 259.0us. The label's size barely moves the cost (135.8us at 100%
     membership against 105.0us at 1%), which is what should have ruled the bitset out
     before the change, not after.
  2. The first two drafts of the unit tests did not reach the folded path AT ALL — 222
     exec tests passed with it poisoned. A bare `Filter(Scan)` seed is claimed earlier in
     the ladder by `try_fused_hop_mask_agg` (which sweeps the whole edge type: right when
     the seed is everything, wrong when it is 1%). The tests needed a SEEK seed, which
     meant running `optimize_indexed` rather than the parsed plan. Poisoning the branch
     and checking which tests notice is what found this; the mutation check is not
     optional here.

## 18. Planner: the equality seed and the conjunct picker had item 9's bug (2026-09-26)

Item 9 gated the RANGE seed on measured selectivity. Sweeping the other three seed
sites for the same shape found it in all of them, plus a second bug in the picker.

- [x] **[high]** engine `opt.rs` — FIXED 2026-09-26. `seek_beats_scan` READ as a
      selectivity test but was one only for ranges. `seed_fraction`'s `Eq` arm returns
      `Some(n)` for ANY bucket size; only `range_count_capped` aborts early, so
      `is_some()` meant "is there an index" for equality and "is it selective" for a
      range. Equality therefore seeded whenever a hash index existed — item 9's bug
      exactly, one branch above item 9's fix. Measured (E75, 200k rows, `dept` = 5
      values):

  ```text
  query                          no index    indexed   ratio
  name = one row (unique)           264.2        0.4   677x    <- the win, kept
  dept = one of 5 (20% pass)        194.6      526.5   0.37x   <- 2.7x SLOWER
  dept = one of 5, GROUP BY         183.6      518.9   0.35x   <- 2.9x SLOWER
  ```

  Now 0.67x / 0.64x. The gate is one predicate (`eq_seek_worth_it`) shared by the
  single-comparison arm and the conjunct picker, so the two spellings of one
  predicate cannot part company.

  A DELIBERATE ASYMMETRY with the range gate: with NO hash index, equality still
  seeds. `index_seek_ids`' fallback is a typed column scan, and it is the FASTEST
  shape available — 194.6us against 291.7us for the `Filter(Scan)` declining would
  leave. Declining would cost time AND give one predicate two plans.

- [x] **[high]** engine `opt.rs` `seed_from_conjuncts` — FIXED 2026-09-26, same commit.
      Two separate faults in one function:

  1. It picked the first seedable conjunct BY POSITION, so conjunct ORDER changed the
     plan: `age >= 30 AND age < 40` seeded the 70% bound, `age < 40 AND age >= 30` the
     40% one — 0.898ms against 0.581ms for two spellings of one range
     (`spelling_probe`, "range AND", a standing ✗ since the probe was written). The
     Gremlin seed layer has ranked by selectivity since
     `the_more_selective_of_two_filters_seeds`; this path was the one still going by
     position. Both spellings are now 0.26ms — the cliff is gone and the group got
     2.2-3.4x faster, because neither broad bound seeds at all now.
  2. Its lowest rung seeded an UNINDEXED range, which makes `RangeSeek` fall back to a
     scan that BOXES every cell. Measured 3878.0us against 3488.6us for the
     `Filter(Scan)` it replaced (E76), and 651.0us against 370.0us on a mixed
     conjunction (E75). The single-comparison arm had been gated on `has_range_index`
     for exactly this reason; the two disagreed. The rung is removed.

  Two plan-shape tests used that rung to observe filter-merging, so they were asserting
  a measured regression. Both now use an equality conjunct, which still seeds.

  SCOPED AND DECLINED 2026-09-27 — see item 27. Original note: `Filter(Scan)` costs ~0.5ns/row more than the
  scan-fallback seek because it materializes the whole batch and then compacts a keep
  list. That is the residual 1.5x a declared index still costs a broad equality.
  Closing it wants a scan that takes a pushed-down predicate — which the scan-fallback
  seek effectively already is — not a different index decision.

## 17. Algorithms saw only an edge's PRIMARY label (2026-09-23)

- [x] **[high]** engine `algo.rs` — FIXED 2026-09-23. A BYTE-IDENTITY BREAK, and an
      internal inconsistency on top of it. The native algorithms filtered
      `edgeLabel` by comparing `want == a.etype`, the edge's PRIMARY type, while the
      query engine has always consulted the whole label set (`edge_carries_wanted`) and
      the pure-TS algorithms have no notion of a primary at all (`Edge.labels` is a
      set). On one edge labelled `[LIKES, KNOWS]`:

  ```text
  native  MATCH (a:N)-[:KNOWS]->(b) RETURN count(*)    2
  ts      degree(edgeLabel: 'KNOWS')                   2
  native  degree(edgeLabel: 'KNOWS')                   1   <- wrong
  ```

  So the same edge was visible to a query and invisible to an algorithm, and the
  two engines disagreed. Six sites: `for_each_nbr` (which most algorithms route
  through), label propagation, and three `type_ok` closures in the shortest-path
  family — all now go through `adj_wanted` / `edge_carries_type`, matching the one
  closure that was already correct. The dead `etype` parameter that the `relax`
  closures no longer need is dropped.

  WHY IT STOOD: no fixture anywhere had a multi-label edge. The algo conformance
  suite compares TS against native across every config, but only over single-label
  graphs, so it could not see this. It now carries a multi-label fixture —
  reverting the fix turns 7 of its tests red.

## 16. GQL: `OPTIONAL` over a match BLOCK is unimplemented (2026-09-21)

- [~] **[med]** engine `gql.rs:1062` — `OPTIONAL MATCH …` parses, but the ISO
  grammar allows `OPTIONAL` over a braced or parenthesized BLOCK of match
  statements, and both are rejected with "expected MATCH after OPTIONAL". Verified
  against the ISO/IEC 39075 BNF digital artifact:

  ```text
  <optional match statement> ::= OPTIONAL <optional operand>
  <optional operand> ::=
      <simple match statement>
    | <left brace> <match statement block> <right brace>
    | <left paren> <match statement block> <right paren>
  <match statement block> ::= <match statement>...
  ```

  So the implemented form is the degenerate single-statement case. The block forms
  matter beyond syntax: `OPTIONAL { MATCH a MATCH b }` is one left-outer over the
  JOINT pattern, which is not expressible as two consecutive `OPTIONAL MATCH`es (that
  is two independent left-outers). A user wanting "either both hops or neither" has
  no spelling today.

  Not a Cypherism — `OPTIONAL MATCH` is genuinely ISO; it shares the spelling with
  Cypher by convergence. Worth confirming because the reverse mistake (assuming a
  Cypher-looking keyword is a Cypherism and removing it) would be worse.

  DIAGNOSED, NOT BUILT (2026-09-21): the block forms now fail with
  `E_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` and a message saying the query is valid ISO, instead of
  `E_SYNTAX` "expected MATCH after OPTIONAL" — which was wrong twice over, since
  `{` IS what the grammar expects there and the user had made no mistake.

  `E_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` rather than `E_UNSUPPORTED` deliberately. The vocabulary
  distinguishes them — "recognized but not yet implemented" vs "a feature that
  isn't supported" — and the difference tells a reader whether to work around it
  forever or wait for it. This engine intends to accept ISO, so it is the former.
  SWEPT 2026-09-21. The engine had no uses of `E_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` at all; it now
  has 15, and the split is:

  - **Reclassified to `E_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`** — Gremlin query parameters (`ffi.rs`,
    whose own comment said "not… yet"), the unbuilt command tiers (whose message
    already read "is not yet implemented"), and 13 parse-time messages in
    `gql.rs`/`gremlin.rs` that literally said "not supported yet" while surfacing
    as **E_SYNTAX** — telling a user they had made a typo when the message itself
    said otherwise.
  - **Left as `E_UNSUPPORTED`** — a branching step inside a filter body
    (`exec.rs`), which tells the caller to RESTRUCTURE, and a map/record property
    in flat PG-text (`codec.rs`), which is a format limitation. Neither is coming.

  (My earlier "12 uses" was a raw grep count including comments and doc lines;
  there were four error-raising sites, two of which moved.)

  `set_exec_error` and both parse-error ladders learned the code, so it survives
  the FFI boundary; verified end-to-end that the prefix is stripped and the
  user-visible message is unchanged. A test pins the distinction in BOTH
  directions — an unbuilt construct must not read as a syntax error, and a real
  syntax error must not claim to be unimplemented.

## 15. Exec: typed filter fast paths panicked on an OPTIONAL null slot (2026-09-21)

- [x] **[high]** engine `exec.rs` — FIXED 2026-09-21, found by `opt/rewrite_fuzz`
      the moment it learned to generate `OptionalExpand`. A miss lands the `u32::MAX`
      null sentinel in the appended slot, and every TYPED fast path in the filter and
      mask layers indexed its column by that id directly (`present[4294967295]`). The
      general `eval_mask` path had always treated it as UNKNOWN and dropped the row, so
      the panic appeared ONLY when the predicate was simple enough to take a fast path
      — the common case.

  Not one site but a CLASS: eleven guards across eight functions
  (`try_filter_keep`, `try_num_disjunction`, `try_num_conjunction`,
  `try_keep_strsearch`, `read_property`, `typed_num_mask`, `typed_str_mask`,
  `typed_strsearch_mask`), including the EDGE arms, since a bound edge is
  `u32::MAX` on a miss too. `index_seek_ids`/`range_seek_ids` take their ids from
  the store and cannot see a sentinel, so they are untouched.

  Reachable from ordinary GQL in two spellings — `WHERE` directly inside `OPTIONAL
MATCH` is rejected by the parser, which is why the obvious spelling was safe:

  ```text
  MATCH (a:N) OPTIONAL MATCH (a)-[:T]->(x) FILTER x.age > 0 RETURN a.name AS n
  MATCH (a:N) OPTIONAL MATCH (a)-[:T]->(x) MATCH (a) WHERE x.age > 0 RETURN a.name AS n
  ```

## 14. Exec: UNION with a narrower right arm panicked (2026-09-19)

- [x] **[high]** engine `exec.rs` — FIXED 2026-09-19, found while teaching
      `opt/rewrite_fuzz` to generate set operations. Reachable from ordinary GQL and a
      PANIC rather than a coded error:

  ```text
  MATCH (a:Person)-[:KNOWS]->(b) RETURN a.name AS x, b.name AS y
  UNION ALL MATCH (n:Person) RETURN n.name AS x
  ```

  The general path had always padded a short arm's rows with NULLs, exactly as
  `Plan::Union` documents and as the TS engine does implicitly (its rows are
  name-keyed, so a missing column simply renders null). The bug was in the
  fast-path GUARD: `variants_agree` indexes the right arm up to the LEFT arm's
  width and was computed EAGERLY, before the `br.slots.len() == ncols` check in the
  `if` that guards it. The out-of-range read happened while deciding whether the
  fast path applied, in a case where it never did.

  Fixed by short-circuiting the width check into `variants_agree`. Regression test
  `union_pads_a_narrower_right_arm` asserts the padding rather than just the
  absence of a panic.

## 13. Exec: an interval-index seek ignored the hop's edge type (2026-09-19)

- [x] **[high]** engine `exec.rs` `interval_expand` — FIXED 2026-09-19, found by
      `opt/rewrite_fuzz` on its first run after learning to generate bound edge
      variables. The RI-tree is keyed on the interval alone and knows nothing about
      edge types; the SEEK path pushed every overlapping edge without consulting
      `want`, while the SCAN path had always filtered correctly via `for_each_nbr`.

  So `MATCH ()-[r:T]->() WHERE r.lo <= $x AND r.hi >= $y` returned edges of EVERY
  type as soon as an interval index existed. **Creating an index changed the
  answer**, silently, on exactly the bitemporal "as of" query the index exists to
  serve ([[bitemporal-interval-index]]).

  Nothing caught it because the bug needs an index AND a type filter together, and
  every hand-written test covering interval overlap had one edge type or no index.
  Fixed with `eid_carries_wanted` (the eid-based twin of `edge_carries_wanted`,
  consulting the whole label set so multi-label edges match identically either
  way), plus a regression test whose fixture puts an A-edge and a B-edge over the
  same interval, so ignoring the type returns exactly twice the rows.

## 12. Planner: Expand pushdown was all-or-nothing, so a label froze a seek (2026-09-17)

- [x] **[high]** engine `opt.rs` — FIXED 2026-09-17, found while checking item 11's
      premise. The predicate-pushdown arm for `Expand` guarded on `refs_below` over the
      WHOLE predicate, so a MIXED conjunction refused to push at all. That is not an
      exotic shape: `(a:Person)-[:KNOWS]->(b:Person) WHERE b.age > 98` lowers to two
      stacked filters, the merge rule fuses them into one `And`, and from then on the
      slot-1 label check pinned the slot-0 range predicate above the hop. The seek never
      happened and the walk ran from every Person.

  **Writing a label made the query 300x slower** — 9382us against 31.6us for the
  same query with the far node left unlabelled. Worst kind of cliff: more
  information given to the planner, worse plan out.

  The fix is the split the VarLength and ShortestPath arms had already been given
  for exactly this reason (opt.rs's own comment: "otherwise a mixed `a.age = 1 AND
b.age = 2` refuses to push at all and the walk runs from every node"). Now 9382us
  -> 446us, and the forwards/backwards spellings optimize to the SAME plan, so the
  probe reports "identical optimized plan" rather than a time that happens to match.

  One trap worth keeping: the arm needs an `any_pushable` match guard. Without it
  it matches EVERY `Expand` and starves the interval-overlap fusion arm below,
  whose predicate reads the bound edge slot and is pushable by nothing — silently
  disabling interval seeks for every bitemporal query. Two tests pin both halves.

  Also fixed here: `spelling_probe`'s fixture had NO index, so every group about
  whether a predicate reaches an index was measuring something else.

# Second pass — 2026-09-01 (correctness / edge-case, deeper lens)

A second sweep of the six substantive packages (gql, gremlin, core, native, sync,
serialization) with a correctness/edge-case lens, told to skip everything above and
find NEW issues. Most findings are CONFIRMED (agent traced input→wrong output, several
ran BOTH engines). **Not yet independently re-verified by me — verify before fixing.**
Almost all the highs are TS-vs-Rust byte-identity breaks the differential fuzzers miss
(they don't generate the triggering inputs: temporal-valued dedup, NaN properties,
reserved-word keys, EXCEPT ALL, etc.).

## Confirmed byte-identity / correctness breaks (high)

- [x] **[high][CONFIRMED]** sync `writelog.ts:107` `since` + `host.ts:616` — a REGRESSED write-log (server restart / failover / fresh node, seq resets to 0) makes `from >= seq` treat a caught-up client as current → returns `[]` not `null` → resync never fires → **every subsequent CDC write silently dropped, unbounded**. Fix: return `[]` only when `from === seq`, `null` when `from > seq`; better, add a log generation/epoch id and resync on change. **← FIXED 2026-09-01**
- [x] **[high][CONFIRMED]** native `graph.ts:1112` — `free()` doesn't consult `state.busy` (every other op gates via `live()`). Freeing / `using`-dispose while an async algo is in flight on the napi backend → **use-after-free** (Rust `command_async` explicitly documents it relies on the facade's single-flight guard). Fix: defer `graphFree` until the pending run settles (a "free requested" flag honored in the algo finally); don't throw (dispose must not throw). **← FIXED 2026-09-01**
- [x] **[high][CONFIRMED]** gql `clauses.ts:1257` — `EXCEPT ALL`/`INTERSECT ALL` don't dedup in TS, but native ALWAYS dedups except/intersect (`ir.rs:442`). Fix: always `distinctRows` for except/intersect; only `union` honors `all`. **← FIXED 2026-09-01**
- [x] **[high][CONFIRMED]** core `Graph.ts:1376` (+`:1642` edges) — `addLabelToVertex` OUTSIDE a tx enforces only required constraints; INSIDE a tx it runs the full suite (type/NOT NULL/unique/validator). Same op, different result; eager path leaves a state `addVertex` would reject. Fix: run the full commit-path check in the eager branch. **← FIXED 2026-09-01**
- [x] **[high][CONFIRMED]** gremlin `dispatch.ts:233` — `dedup()` falls back to REFERENCE identity for class-instance values, so equal-valued temporals never dedup (`values('vf').dedup()`: TS 3, native 2). `group`/`groupCount` key them by value correctly (inconsistent). Fix: broaden the composite test to include temporals (`tupleKey` already handles them). **← FIXED 2026-09-01**
- [x] **[high][CONFIRMED]** serialization non-finite — **FIXED 2026-09-01** (pg-text/csv → `null`, byte-matching Rust; graphson unchanged = shared limitation; normalizeValue stays first-class). Was: serialization `value.ts:95` — non-finite (`NaN`/`±Inf`) no longer coerced to `null`, contradicting value.ts's own docstring + `core/validate.ts`. graphson decode THROWS on a NaN prop; csv `Inf→NaN`; pg-text number→string. **POLICY DECISION**: is non-finite first-class (per `errors.test.ts:75`) or coerced-to-null (per the docstrings + [[numeric-model-f64]])? graphson throwing is a bug either way.

## Confirmed (med)

- [x] **[med][CONFIRMED]** gql `executor.ts:892` — simple `CASE subject WHEN v` uses `s === wv` (JS strict) not value-equality; native lowers to `subject = v`. `CASE date(x) WHEN date(x)` → TS 'no', native 'yes'. Fix: `structuralEq`. **← FIXED 2026-09-01**
- [x] **[med][CONFIRMED]** gql `shortcuts.ts:387` — reachability DISTINCT fast path keys rows as `cells.map(valueKey).join('')` (no separator) → two string columns collide, dropping distinct rows. Fix: `join('\x01')` (match `rowKeyOf`). **← FIXED 2026-09-01**
- [x] **[med][CONFIRMED]** core `Graph.ts:1002` (clone) / `:1102` (copy) — `new Graph()` with no options drops `this.settings`: clone reverts clock, limits, parallelism to defaults. Fix: carry settings across. **← FIXED 2026-09-01**
- [x] **[med][CONFIRMED]** native `backend-engine.ts:387` — `prepare` ignored `maxOperatorChain` (advertised by the contract + docs, threaded down, but never forwarded). FIXED as part of the operator-chain unification: the `prepare` command payload is now JSON `{query, maxDepth?}` and `parse_prepared_limited` honors the per-prepare ceiling. This also surfaced a deeper gap — the configurable `operatorChain` limit was **entirely dead in the native engine** (never read; the parser used a hardcoded `MAX_EXPR_DEPTH=128`), so `new Graph({ maxOperatorChain })` was a no-op for ALL native queries, and native rejected 129–10k-term chains that TS accepted. Unified both engines onto ONE `operatorChain` knob (bounds nesting depth AND flat-chain length), default **1024** (was 10k; capped ~2× under TS's ~1900-deep JS-stack recursion ceiling so the guard fires as a clean coded error on every runtime — measured: TS ~1900, wasm ~3830, bun native ~32768). Native query path reads `store.limits().operator_chain`; TS parser's two guards (`MAX_DEPTH`/`MAX_CHAIN`) now share the one knob via `DEFAULT_CONFIG`.

## Lower / suspected

- [x] **[med][CONFIRMED]** serialization `csv/index.ts:466` — a string LIST element beginning `\T…` decodes as a type-override sigil (`['\Ti:5']` → `[5]`). Scalar-string path is unaffected. **FIXED** (both codecs): a `\T…` string element is emitted as an explicit `\Ts:` string-code override, decoding back to the literal. Rust `lenke-codec/src/csv.rs` had the identical bug (so it was byte-identical, not a divergence) — fixed in lockstep to stay byte-identical. Regression tests both sides; codec-fuzz + backend-parity (fresh wasm) green.
- [x] **[med][SUSPECTED]** sync dedupe capacity overrun — FIXED (2026-09-16), and CONFIRMED rather than suspected: the global FIFO was unsafe at any size, since a quiet client holding unacked writes keeps its replay window open while others flood past capacity and evict its ids. Replaced with per-session windows that drain on the client's own `ackedThrough` (piggybacked on each write) and a loud `E_RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` NACK when a session's window is genuinely full — the client already surfaces that as a rejected `mutate()`. Sizing was never the fix: only the client's ack can distinguish "old" from "no longer needed".
- [x] **[low][CONFIRMED]** core `Graph.ts:1894` — unique-constraint declare-time scan uses a JS Set (NaN===NaN) while runtime enforcement excludes NaN → declare-vs-insert ordering changes the outcome. Fix: skip non-indexable values in the declare scan. **← FIXED 2026-09-01**
- [x] **[low][CONFIRMED]** serialization `equality.ts:6` — FIXED (2026-09-10): preserveNonFinite replacer distinguishes NaN/±Inf/-0 from null/0 — `graphContentEqual` canon via `JSON.stringify` maps NaN/±Inf/null/-0 all to null → the conformance verifier can't catch non-finite corruption (why #1's csv bug passes). Fix: non-finite-preserving structural equality in the conformance path.
- [x] **[low][CONFIRMED]** native `backend-wasm-engine.ts:221` — FIXED (2026-09-10): readLastError frees errPtr in a finally (+`:278`,`:156`) — crate buffer freed after `readBytes` with no try/finally → wasm-heap leak if `readBytes` throws (broken result contract). FFI twin uses finally. Fix: free in finally.
- [x] **[low][CONFIRMED]** sync `host.ts:653` — `StatusMessage.connected` hard-coded true, never flipped → `getStatus().connected` stale-true during an outage. **FIXED by dropping the field**: a status message only arrives while the wire is up, so the boolean carried no information (a bound client can't observe its own transport dying — that's what `createReconnectingClient().connected()`/`onConnectivity()` are for). Removed from `StatusMessage`, host, client state + JSDoc, and tests; `getStatus()` now returns `{ pendingWrites }`.
- [x] **[low][CONFIRMED]** sync `reconnect.ts:189` — ALREADY FIXED (verified 2026-09-10, reconnect.ts:216-225 closes on the sync-close path) — a transport firing `closed()` synchronously during `connect()` leaks the connection handle (`held.c` assigned after `connect()` returns). Fix: assign before, or close on the sync-close path.
- [x] **[low][SUSPECTED]** native `graph.ts` store `snapshotOf` — NOT A BUG (verified 2026-09-10): fingerprint is set AFTER cell.run() — advances `seenFingerprint` before `cell.run()`, so a throwing run caches nothing yet marks the version seen → stale snapshot masks recovery. Fix: set fingerprint after run.
- [x] **[low][CONFIRMED]** gremlin `filters.ts:51` — `where(pred)` error message lists `within`/`without` as supported, then rejects them (they carry `values` not `value`). Fix: drop them from the message or handle them. **← FIXED 2026-09-01**
- [x] **[low][SUSPECTED]** gremlin `iteration.ts:258` — ALREADY FIXED (verified 2026-09-10): branchStep keys options via tupleKey (value-equality) — `branch()` option match uses identity though the comment claims deep-equality → value-typed option keys misroute. Fix: key options through value-normalization (or fix the comment).
- [x] **[low][CONFIRMED]** native `backend-ffi-engine.ts:152` — query params uses raw `ptr(p)` not the zero-length `bytesPtr` guard its siblings use → empty-but-non-null params throw (latent; facade collapses empty→null). **FIXED**: now uses `bytesPtr(p)`, matching every sibling call site.
- [ ] **[low→overlap]** core `Graph.ts:2133` valueType also swallows LocalTime/ZonedTime/ZonedDateTime → extends the already-logged Date/Map item to first-class temporals.

## Snapshot helpers (checked 2026-09-01) — WON'T CONSOLIDATE (user)

Three distinct mechanisms, not one duplicated helper: native `store.ts` `snapshotOf`
(version + epoch-sum fingerprint, reference-cached rows over a RustGraph), react
`snapshotGate.ts` `nextSnapshot` (epoch-sum fingerprint + `isEqual` over a core Graph;
`useGraphSelector` delegates to it), and sync `client.ts` `getSnapshot` (push-driven,
not fingerprint-based). The only literal overlap is the one-line epoch-sum reduce, and
extracting it would add a `@lenke/native`→`@lenke/core` edge for a trivial helper.
Justified parallelism for two graph backends + one unrelated push snapshot — leave it.

# Third pass — 2026-09-01 (fresh discovery, post-csv)

Prompted by the csv `\T` list-element bug: hunt its siblings (sentinel/escape
collisions the JSON.stringify verifier is blind to) and verify the still-open
SUSPECTED items. All four below are CONFIRMED with a concrete repro.

- [x] **[med][CONFIRMED]** engine (Rust) lacks the element-name validation the TS `Graph` enforces. Native ndjson ingest ACCEPTS a `::` label, an empty label `[""]`, and an empty property key `""` — all three of which `core/validate.ts` (`validateElementNames`, wired into addVertex/addEdge AND deserialize) rejects in TS. So the SAME document is rejected by TS and accepted by Rust (accept/reject divergence). Worse, a native-ingested `::` label encodes to graphson `"label":"a::b"`, which any decoder splits into `["a","b"]` (single→multi-label corruption); an empty label round-trips to `[]` (loss). Fix: add the same element-name guard to the engine's ingest path. [verified: all three accepted over FFI]
- [x] **[med][CONFIRMED]** serialization pg-json + ndjson (BOTH engines agree → verifier-blind) — a first-class record/map whose single key is a temporal tag with a string value decodes as a Temporal, not a record: `LenkeRecord.from([['@date','2024-01-15']])` round-trips to a `LocalDate`. Silent type corruption. Rust `lib.rs:92`/`json.rs:90`; TS `value.ts:137`/`temporal.ts:691`. Divergence sub-case: an invalid ISO string (`{"@date":"nope"}`) → TS throws at decode, Rust keeps an unvalidated Temporal. graphson is immune (needs an `@type` wrapper). Fix: disambiguate a record from a tagged temporal on the wire (e.g. a record marker), or accept as a documented limitation. [verified via LenkeRecord round-trip]
- [x] **[low-med][CONFIRMED]** native store `snapshotOf` (`store.ts:173`,`:191-192`) advances `seenVersion` + `seenFingerprint` BEFORE `cell.run()`; a `run()` that throws leaves them advanced but `cached` stale, so the next same-version read hits the `v === seenVersion` early-return and silently returns STALE data instead of re-throwing. A live query that starts failing after a mutation (e.g. a CAST error on newly-inserted data) throws once, then serves outdated rows under `useSyncExternalStore`. Fix: advance the bookkeeping only after `run()` succeeds. [verified: read2 throws, read3 returns stale []]
- [x] **[low][CONFIRMED]** gremlin `branch()` (`iteration.ts:258`) matches options with `Object.is || ===` (reference equality) though the comment (`iteration.ts:235`) claims "deep-equality on `match`" → value-typed option keys (list/map/temporal) misroute to `default`/`none`, diverging from lenke's value-equality model and TinkerPop (Java `.equals`). Fix: value-normalize the match key, or correct the comment. [confirmed by inspection]

## Engine (Rust) robustness — third pass, 2026-09-01

- [x] **[HIGH][CONFIRMED]** engine `gql.rs` + `gremlin.rs` — the recursive-descent parsers have NO nesting-depth cap (only _traversal_/operator-chain depth is limited, which is post-parse and too late). A plain query string of deeply nested structure overflows the stack and ABORTS the process: `RETURN [[[…1…]]]` at depth ~6000 (~12 KB) → SIGSEGV [verified: exit 139; depth 3000 fine]; also `RETURN ((((1))))`, `NOT NOT … true`, and Gremlin `where(__.where(__.where(…)))`. Uncatchable — parse runs OUTSIDE the `ffi.rs` `guarded` catch_unwind (`ffi.rs:573/604/641`), and a stack overflow isn't recoverable by `catch_unwind` anyway. Native: process abort (DoS for an embedded/server host). wasm: the "unreachable" trap that kills the REPL. The same unbounded recursion is also in the post-parse consumers (`optimize_indexed`, `exec` expr `eval`). Fix: a hard nesting-depth cap in BOTH parsers that rejects with `E_SYNTAX`/`E_RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` before building the tree; optionally a query-length cap in `ffi.rs` (there is none today).
- [x] **[low][CONFIRMED]** engine `algo.rs` — algorithm CALL/direct config has no cap on `iterations` or `threads`. `{iterations: 1e18}` saturates to `u32::MAX` (~4.29e9) fixed iterations → hang/DoS (not a panic); a huge `threads` asks rayon for that many OS threads (falls back to inline on builder failure, so no fault, but churns first). Reachable via the algorithm surface. Fix: clamp both to sane maxima.

**Audited and found SOLID (no reachable defect)** — recorded so it isn't re-derived: scalar string fns (`exec/scalar.rs` — saturating*add/.min/saturating casts, parser-enforced arity), list/path/record subscripts (`exec/evaluator.rs` — bounds+integer checked → NULL), LIMIT/SKIP (non-negative-int checked, saturating slicing), NDJSON ingest (empty edge labels rejected before `labels[0]`), temporal arithmetic (`checked*_`/`try_from`), algo node indexing (bounds-checked src/pivots/betweenness). The FFI read/write paths correctly use the fallible `try_run_`under`guarded`; the one gap is that PARSING precedes and sits outside that net.

### Fixed 2026-09-02 (all six of the third pass + engine pass)

Landed as six logical commits (+ one examples fixup), each with regression tests;
`bun run check`, both crates' clippy, all seven byte-identity fuzzers, and the
serialization / sync / native / gql / gremlin suites are green.

- **[HIGH] parser stack overflow** — a 128-deep nesting cap in the GQL + Gremlin
  parsers (rejects with E_RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED before the stack overflows / the tree is
  built); the TS `@lenke/gql` cap is aligned to 128 for accept/reject parity.
- **[med] engine element-name guards** — `validate_label`/`validate_prop_key` enforced
  on every codec + ndjson ingest boundary (E_INVALID_VALUE), matching TS.
- **[med] record vs tagged temporal** — `@`-leading record keys escaped on the JSON
  wire (`@date` → `@@date`) in all three engines, byte-identical.
- **[low-med] snapshotOf** — bookkeeping advances only after `run()` succeeds, so a
  failing live query re-throws instead of serving stale rows.
- **[low] gremlin branch()** — option keys match by value (`tupleKey`), matching
  TinkerPop (verified against gremlin-console).
- **[low] algo iterations/threads** — clamped to sane maxima (`iterations: 0` still a
  no-op, matching TS).

# Fourth pass — 2026-09-02 (correctness discovery: paths, tx, index-seeding)

Two parallel correctness hunts (index-seeding, transactions) + direct cross-engine
probes of aggregation / strings / temporal / paths / syntax. All below are CONFIRMED
with a repro; in every divergence native is the correct/robust side.

- [x] **[HIGH][CONFIRMED]** native host `transaction()` ABORTS the process when nested. `backend.ts:171` documents "nesting joins the outer frame" and the TS engine does exactly that (inner joins outer; outer rollback discards inner writes). The native path has NO depth tracking: a second `beginTransaction` → `store.begin()` hits `assert!(self.undo.is_none(), "nested transactions are not supported")` (`store.rs:4093`), a non-unwinding panic ACROSS the FFI boundary → `abort()` — an uncatchable whole-process crash [verified: `g.transaction(() => g.transaction(...))` triggers a Bun crash report]. wasm shares the path (REPL-killing "unreachable"). The ISO-keyword path `run_tx_control` guards this gracefully with `E_INVALID_GRAPH_OP`; only the host API is unguarded. Fix: depth-count the host tx (join-outer like TS) or return `E_INVALID_GRAPH_OP` instead of asserting.
- [x] **[med][CONFIRMED]** GQL reserved words can't be dot-accessed as property/field names in the TS engine but can in native. `RETURN n.count` / `n.date` / `n.value` / `n.size` / `n.year` / `n.month` / `n.order` / `n.group` / `n.start` / `n.end` / `n.min` / `n.max` / … (23 common words) → `E_SYNTAX` in `@lenke/gql`, but return the value in native — for a direct property (`n.count`) AND a nested record field (`n.rec.date`). Accept/reject divergence on common property names. TS workaround is backtick-quoting (`` n.`count` ``; `n['count']` is map-indexing and returns null on a node). Native accepts keywords as member names (like JS `obj.count`). Fix: allow a reserved word as the property name after `.` in the TS parser (match native), or decide native should also require delimiting.
- [x] **[med][CONFIRMED]** `version`/`epoch` diverge on a ROLLED-BACK tx: native advances them by 1 (the forward `touch()`/`bump_epoch` are never reverted by the undo replay), TS leaves them unchanged. Stored values are correct on both (no corruption), but a rolled-back native tx spuriously invalidates every reactive-store subscriber (fingerprint bump) while TS notifies none — relevant to `@lenke/sync` replica invalidation and React `version`/`epoch` subscriptions. Fix: snapshot/restore version+epoch across a rollback, or don't bump until commit.
- [x] **[low][CONFIRMED]** `{0,0}` quantified path returns 1-hop rows in TS. `MATCH (a)-[:R]->{0,0}(b)` must match exactly zero hops (b = start); native returns only the start, TS returns start + 1-hop neighbours (as if `{0,1}`). Root cause: `matching.ts:1134` emit condition checks only the lower bound (`rep2 >= min`), never the upper — the unconditionally-generated first hop slips through when `max=0`. Fix: `&& (top.max === null || rep2 <= top.max)`.
- [x] **[low-med][CONFIRMED]** schema DDL (createIndex / createUniqueConstraint / …) run INSIDE a tx that then ABORTS persists — the tx's data writes revert but its schema op does not (verified: the index survives + the constraint still enforces). Holds identically on BOTH engines (DDL implicit-commit), so not a divergence, but undocumented rollback residue / a footgun. Fix: transaction-log schema ops, or document the split.

**Audited CLEAN (no wrong-answer bug found) — recorded so it isn't re-derived:** the
index-seeding / seek layer (~130 checks: value edge cases incl. null/NaN/±Inf/-0/big-int
as seek keys, type-mismatch, absent-key, equivalent spellings, hash+range+interval+
bitemporal seeks, dict-encoded columns at scale, index consistency after SET/INSERT/merge,
the Gremlin `has()` lowering — every seek matched a full scan); aggregation with
null/NaN/mixed-type/empty-group; string/unicode/numeric functions; temporal
compare/arith/order; OPTIONAL MATCH null propagation; CAST; and the tx UNDO machinery
itself (every mutation kind, index consistency, deferred-constraint abort, CDC discard).

### Fixed 2026-09-02 (fourth-pass #1, #2, #4)

Three logical commits, each with regression tests; `bun run check`, differential-fuzz,
the full fuzzer sweep, and the gql (516) / native (707) suites are green.

- **[HIGH] nested transaction crash** — depth-tracked in the shared graph wrapper: only
  the outermost begin/commit reaches the backend (nested joins the outer flat frame),
  fixing FFI + wasm with no Rust change. Matches TS + the documented contract.
- **[med] reserved words as property names** — a `memberName()` parser helper accepts a
  keyword after `.` (and in map keys / SET / PROPERTY_EXISTS), case-preserved via the
  keyword token's `raw`. Binding positions still reject reserved words.
- **[low] {0,0} quantifier** — the walk's emit condition now also checks the upper bound.

STILL OPEN (awaiting a direction decision): #3 (version/epoch advance on a rolled-back
native tx) and #5 (schema DDL persists through an aborted tx, both engines).

### Fixed 2026-09-03 (fourth-pass #3, #5)

- **[med] #3 version/epoch on rollback** — the native store snapshots `version` + `epochs`
  at `begin` and restores them at the end of `rollback`, so a rolled-back tx no longer
  advances the reactive counters (TS was already correct). Regression test.
- **[low-med] #5 schema ops in a transaction** — BOTH engines now revert a
  createIndex/constraint/validator/invariant (and a drop) declared in a transaction that
  aborts. Native: a declarative-schema snapshot at the single `apply_schema_op` hook,
  restored on rollback. TS: the same snapshot (reusing `#copyRegistriesInto`) captured at
  each schema method, restored in `applyUndoAndReset` after the data undos. Only the schema
  DELTA is reverted; pre-existing index buckets are kept consistent by the data undos.
  Regression tests both engines.

The whole fourth pass is now resolved (index-seeding was already clean).

# Fifth pass — 2026-09-03 (Gremlin-vs-TinkerPop, egress/reload, writes)

Two hunts (Gremlin vs real TinkerPop ground truth via podman; egress/reload surfaces) +
direct write/`_MERGE` probes. All CONFIRMED with a repro. NB several "both engines agree"
Gremlin items turned out to be native-vs-TS divergences the gremlin-fuzz misses (it never
generates the triggering shapes).

- [x] **[HIGH][CONFIRMED]** native prepared-statement WRITES are a silent no-op. `prepare("INSERT …").query()` returns `[]`, applies nothing, throws nothing (verified for INSERT/SET/DETACH DELETE/REMOVE, with params/literals, even inside `transaction()`). Root cause: `ffi.rs` `prepared_run` (~L979) calls `exec::try_run`/`try_run_gql_json` DIRECTLY, bypassing `exec::run_query` — the dispatcher that owns the write/read split (the normal `query` path uses it at L634). A write plan handed to the read executor produces no rows and no mutation. `prepare`'s docstring promises results "identical" to `query()`. Prepared parameterized INSERT is the canonical bulk-load use → every row silently lost. Fix: route `prepared_run` through `run_query`, or reject write plans with a coded error.
- [x] **[HIGH][CONFIRMED]** auto-commit `mergeNdjson` bypasses constraint enforcement. Merging (outside a transaction) into a graph with `UNIQUE(Person.email)` loads two `dup@x.com` nodes (`nodesAdded:2`) — the unique index holds two rows under one key while the constraint is still live (a later normal INSERT of that email throws `E_CONSTRAINT_VIOLATION`): internally inconsistent, unreachable by any constrained write. `REQUIRED`/`TYPE` are bypassed too. `transaction(g => g.mergeNdjson(...))` CORRECTLY rejects + rolls back — so bare auto-commit merge skips the deferred-constraint recheck that an auto-commit INSERT runs as an implicit checked tx. Doubly important: `mergeNdjson` is the "COPY FROM" append API AND the recommended snapshot-reload path (schema-first-then-append) → a reload silently skips validation. Fix: run the deferred checks on the auto-commit merge path (mirror the INSERT auto-commit).
- [x] **[med-high][CONFIRMED]** Gremlin `min()`/`max()` on STRING values: native does lexicographic (`josh`/`ripple`, MATCHES TinkerPop), TS throws `E_INVALID_VALUE`. Native-vs-TS divergence; TS is over-restrictive (lenke's own total-order sort/min/max policy covers strings). Common query. Fix TS to total-order min/max like native.
- [x] **[med][CONFIRMED]** `_MERGE` tail grammar diverges — **RETURN half fixed 2026-09-04.** Native `_MERGE (…) RETURN <items>` now works (read-after-write tail, byte-identical to TS create + update paths). A bare trailing `SET` (and other WRITE clauses) after `_MERGE` still composes in TS only — that is a broader structural gap (native `_MERGE` is a leading command with a read tail, not a full pipeline clause), deprioritized: a bare `SET` is the redundant/verbose spelling of putting the value in the pattern (which `_MERGE` already sets). See "Remaining: `_MERGE` write-clause composition" below.
- [x] **[med][CONFIRMED]** Gremlin `valueMap(true)` (the id/label token form) — **fixed 2026-09-04.** Both engines implement TinkerPop's includeTokens overload byte-identically: a leading boolean prepends `id` + `label` (like `elementMap()`, no edge IN/OUT); `valueMap(true, 'k')` keeps tokens + filters; plain `valueMap()` unchanged. Native json_out fast path + value-tree evaluator both covered.
- [x] **[med][FIXED 2026-09-05]** Gremlin `by('key')` on an ABSENT property now FILTERS the traverser (order/group/groupCount/dedup/select) or OMITS the key (project), matching TinkerPop, byte-identically in both engines; a stored present-`null` is kept. See "Fixed 2026-09-05: `by(absent)` filter semantics" below.
- [x] **[low-med][CONFIRMED]** native `lastWriteScope(key)` binding drops the fail-open flag — **fixed 2026-09-04.** `graph.lastWriteScope` now returns `{ scopes, open }` (both the public API + the backend contract); `@lenke/sync` reads `open` and collapses it into the routing filter's existing fail-open path. Backend-engine test (FFI + wasm) covers scoped / missing-key / edge cases.
- [x] **[low][CONFIRMED]** Gremlin `repeat(out()).times(0)` runs 0 iterations (while-do) vs TinkerPop's do-while (1) — **documented 2026-09-04** (decision: keep lenke's intuitive count semantics, both engines agree; comment in `iteration.ts` records the deviation). No behavior change.
- [x] **[low][FIXED 2026-09-05]** Gremlin `math('a + b')` reading `project()`/`group()` map keys — both engines now resolve a named math variable from the current Map row (TinkerPop MathStep scoping): `project('a','b').by('age').by('age').math('a + b')` → 58 in both. An unbound name on a non-map frontier stays E_INVALID_VALUE. (The original repro's `by(constant(1))` also hit a separate native `project().by(constant())` gap — orthogonal to math; noted for later.)

**Audited CLEAN (no bug):** full snapshot round-trip completeness (multi-label nodes+edges,
null-vs-absent, temporal, list, record props, all constraint kinds, hash+interval indexes,
validators/invariants — byte-identical reload BOTH orders, reloaded graph still enforces +
indexes; only gap is the auto-commit-merge above); Arrow IPC egress (native `queryArrowIpc`
≡ JS `toArrowIPC` byte-identical across scalars/nulls/strings/lists/records/temporals/empty);
prepared READS (≡ direct query across every param type, rebinding, indexed seeks); CDC scope
derivation (aside from the dropped flag); ORDER BY/DISTINCT/LIMIT/GROUP BY with
null/mixed/list keys; SET/REMOVE/label-mutation/DETACH DELETE/`SET +=`/computed props.

### Fixed 2026-09-04 (fifth-pass: the two HIGH silent-data bugs + min/max)

- **[HIGH] prepared writes** + **[HIGH] auto-commit mergeNdjson** — one commit: both write
  paths now route through `exec::run_query` / a checked transaction (prepared writes apply;
  merge runs the deferred constraint recheck + rolls back on violation). Tests on FFI+wasm.
- **[med-high] Gremlin min/max on strings** — TS drops the numeric-only guard; min/max now
  total-order any value like native + TinkerPop. gremlin-fuzz confirms native≡TS.

ALL RESOLVED (2026-09-04/05): `_MERGE … RETURN` (RETURN half; write-clause composition
deprioritized), `valueMap(true)`, `by(absent)` filtering, `lastWriteScope` open-flag API,
`repeat().times(0)` (documented), math-over-project. The only threads left are the two
deprioritized/orthogonal gaps flagged inline: native `_MERGE`/`INSERT` as full pipeline
clauses, and native `project().by(constant(...))`.

### Fixed 2026-09-04 (the case-by-case batch)

After the fifth-pass decisions, this batch landed as logical commits:

- **`lastWriteScope` open-flag API** — `graph.lastWriteScope` returns `{ scopes, open }`; sync
  consumer updated; FFI+wasm test. (`feat(native): lastWriteScope returns the open fail-open flag`)
- **`repeat().times(0)`** — documented the while-do deviation in `iteration.ts` (no behavior
  change). (`docs(gremlin): document the repeat().times(0) deviation from TinkerPop`)
- **`valueMap(true)`** — TinkerPop includeTokens implemented byte-identically in both engines,
  json_out fast path + value-tree evaluator. (`feat(gremlin): implement valueMap(true) includeTokens…`)
- **`_MERGE … RETURN`** — native gained the read-after-write RETURN tail (matches TS create +
  update). (`feat(engine): _MERGE ... RETURN in native, matching TS`)
- Plus a `chore(core)` clearing three pre-existing oxlint findings that had landed without a
  lint run.

All seven byte-identity fuzzers green after the batch.

### Remaining: `_MERGE` write-clause composition (deprioritized)

TS parses the clause pipeline uniformly, so ANY clause composes after `_MERGE` (`_MERGE … SET …`,
`_MERGE … MATCH … RETURN`, `MATCH … _MERGE …`). Native treats `_MERGE` (like `INSERT`) as a
statement-leading write command with only a read tail (now incl. RETURN). So `_MERGE … SET …`
is accepted in TS (`SET` yields no rows) and `E_SYNTAX` in native. Per the user, a bare `SET`
after `_MERGE` is the redundant/verbose spelling (put the value in the pattern — `_MERGE` already
sets it), so this is deprioritized. A full fix means making native `_MERGE`/`INSERT` first-class
composable pipeline clauses — a larger structural change, separate from the RETURN work. Not
fuzzer-covered (the differential fuzzer does not generate `_MERGE`).

### Fixed 2026-09-05: `by(absent)` filter semantics

**DONE** (`feat(gremlin): by(absent) filters the traverser (TinkerPop), both engines`). A keying
`by('k')` over an ABSENT property now FILTERS the traverser (order/group/groupCount/dedup/select) or
OMITS the key (project), byte-identically across both engines; a stored present-`null` is a value and
is kept. TS uses a `NO_VALUE` sentinel from `evalBy` (via `Object.hasOwn`, which also stops an
inherited name like `toString` leaking a prototype fn); native installs a `PROPERTY_EXISTS` gate at
Gremlin lowering (guarded by `by_absent_gates` so Map/scalar frontiers, where `by('k')` is a field
read, are untouched) and a `MapLit { omit_absent }` flag for project (evaluator arm + fused JSON fast
path share `prop_present_mask`). Also fixed the `planToGremlin` emitter dropping `dedup().by(...)`.
Verified by a cross-engine test over every keying step + the present-null-vs-absent distinction,
native Rust tests, and the full cargo suite + all seven fuzzers. Original spec/ground-truth retained
below.

**Decision (user): match TinkerPop — a `by()` that yields no value acts as a filter.** This is the
last non-punted item and is bigger than "add a filter": `evalBy` feeds ~15 call sites across 6 TS
files plus the entire native by-evaluation, it must distinguish ABSENT (filter/omit) from a stored
present-`null` (keep), and it reconciles several pre-existing TS/native divergences at once. By(absent)
is exactly the case the fuzzers do NOT cover (why these bugs survived), so it needs hand-written
cross-engine tests per step, not fuzzer reliance. Discovery is done — the tables below are the spec.
